```python
def test_scorer_rejects_model_with_wrong_popularity_shape():
    """Scorer should reject model where popularity length != num_items."""
    model = _create_mock_model()
    # Manually corrupt the model
    model.scores = np.ones(20)  # Wrong length

    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="popularity array has length"):
        BaselineScorer(model)
```

```python
def test_scorer_rejects_model_with_wrong_user_items_shape():
    """Scorer should reject model where user_items columns != num_items."""
    # Use popularity model (no similarity) to simplify testing
    model = _create_mock_model(with_similarity=False)
    # Manually corrupt the model - change num_items but not user_items, also update popularity
    original_num_items = model.num_items
    model.num_items = original_num_items + 10  # Set to a different value
    model.scores = np.ones(model.num_items)  # Match new num_items

    # Should raise ValueError for inconsistency
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        BaselineScorer(model)
```

```python
def test_scorer_rejects_model_with_nan_similarity():
    """Scorer should reject model with NaN in similarity."""
    model = _create_mock_model()
    # Inject NaN into similarity (ensure there's data to corrupt)
    if model.similarity.nnz > 0:
        model.similarity.data[0] = np.nan
    else:
        # If similarity is empty, add some data with NaN
        model.similarity = sparse.csr_matrix(([np.nan], ([0], [1])), shape=(model.num_items, model.num_items))

    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="non-finite values"):
        BaselineScorer(model)


    # At least should be a set, specific items depend on interactions
```

```python
def test_scorer_zero_peak_popularity():
    """Scorer should handle case where all popularity scores are zero."""
    model = _create_mock_model()
    model.scores = np.zeros(model.num_items)
    scorer = BaselineScorer(model)

    # Should not crash, popularity normalization should handle this
    item_idxs = np.array([0, 1, 2])
    scores = scorer.score(user_idx=0, item_idxs=item_idxs)
    assert scores.shape == (3,)
```

```python
def test_formula_is_cf_plus_tiny_normalised_popularity(world):
    knn = ItemKNNRecommender(neighbors=5).fit(world.interactions)
    scorer = BaselineScorer(knn)
    scores = scorer.score(1, np.arange(6))  # u1 saw item 0
    cf = np.array([0, 4 / np.sqrt(20), 2 / np.sqrt(10), 1 / np.sqrt(5), 4 / np.sqrt(20), 3 / np.sqrt(15)])
    # legacy orientation: (history @ S)[i] = S[0, i]; neighbours are all kept, so this equals sim(0, i)
    assert scores == pytest.approx(cf + np.array([5, 4, 2, 1, 4, 3]) / 5 * 1e-6, abs=1e-7)
```

```python
def test_equal_cf_scores_are_ordered_by_popularity():
    sim = np.zeros((3, 3)); sim[0, 1] = sim[0, 2] = 0.5
    scorer = BaselineScorer(FakeModel(sim, [1, 1, 100], [[1, 0, 0]]))
    s = scorer.score(0, np.array([1, 2]))
    assert s[1] > s[0]  # true tie in CF -> the more popular item wins
```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("gap, popularity_wins", [(1e-3, False), (1e-5, False), (1e-7, True)])
def test_popularity_is_a_NEAR_tie_breaker_not_an_exact_one(gap, popularity_wins):
    """Documents the real behaviour: the popularity term is <= 1e-6, so it overrides CF only when the CF scores
    differ by less than that. Item 1 has the higher CF score (0.5 + gap); item 2 is far more popular."""

    scorer = BaselineScorer(FakeModel(sim, [1, 1, 1000], [[1, 0, 0]]))
    s = scorer.score(0, np.array([1, 2]))
    assert bool(s[1] > s[0]) == popularity_wins
```

```python
def test_positive_cf_below_the_popularity_term_loses_to_a_popular_zero_cf_item():
```

```python
def test_scores_have_input_shape_and_are_finite(world):
    scorer = BaselineScorer(ItemKNNRecommender(neighbors=5).fit(world.interactions))
    for idxs in (np.array([], dtype=np.int64), np.array([3]), np.array([5, 5, 0, -1, 99])):
        out = scorer.score(2, idxs)
        assert out.shape == idxs.shape and np.isfinite(out).all()
```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("query, tokens", [
    ("quiet keyboard", ["quiet", "keyboard"]),
    ("USB-C cable", ["usb-c", "cable"]),
    ("keyboard keyboard KEYBOARD", ["keyboard"]),  # duplicates collapse, order kept
    ("the best cheap keyboard for me", ["keyboard"]),  # stopwords dropped
    ("keyboard under 50 dollars", ["keyboard", "dollars"]),  # 'under' stopword, pure number dropped
    ("123 4.5 1,000 $20", []),  # numbers only
    ("4k tv 2tb ssd 1080p 20-inch", ["4k", "tv", "2tb", "ssd", "1080p", "20-inch"]),  # digit-leading model tokens kept whole
    ('say "hello" OR AND NOT (x*)', ["say", "hello", "not"]),  # quotes / FTS operators are just punctuation
    ("a b c", []),  # single letters
    ("", []),
])
def test_tokenizer(query, tokens):
    assert _tokenize(query) == tokens
```

```python
    first = [c["item_id"] for c in tool(query="same words").data["candidates"]]
```

```python
def test_search_applies_structured_filters(kb_corpus):
    tool = SemanticSearchTool(kb_corpus)
    cheap = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"price_max": 20})
    assert _ids(cheap) == ["Electronics::k3"]
    games = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"domain": "Video_Games"})
    assert _ids(games) == ["Video_Games::g1"]
```

```python
def test_search_query_cannot_inject_fts_syntax(kb_corpus):
    tool = SemanticSearchTool(kb_corpus)
    for q in ['keyboard" OR title:mouse', "keyboard NOT mouse *", "NEAR(keyboard mouse)", "col:keyboard"]:
        assert tool(query=q).ok  # treated as plain words, never as FTS5 operators
```

```python
def test_search_hyphenated_tokens_preserved(kb_corpus):
    """Test that hyphenated tokens like 'usb-c' are preserved."""
    meta_usb = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "parent_asin": ["u1", "u2"],
            "domain": ["Electronics", "Electronics"],
            "title": ["USB-C Cable", "USB Cable"],
            "price": [10.0, 15.0],
            "average_rating": [4.5, 4.0],
            "rating_number": [100, 50],
            "store": ["StoreA", "StoreB"],
            "main_category": ["Cables", "Cables"],
            "categories": [["Cables"], ["Cables"]],
        }
    )
    corpus_usb = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta_usb)
    result = SemanticSearchTool(corpus_usb)(query="usb-c cable")
    assert "Electronics::u1" in _ids(result)
```

```python
def test_search_empty_lists_in_filters(kb_corpus):
    """Test that empty lists in filters are treated as 'no filter'."""
    tool = SemanticSearchTool(kb_corpus)
    result_empty_any = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"categories_any": []})
    result_empty_all = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"categories_all": []})
    result_empty_domain = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"domain": []})
    # All should return results, not filter out everything
    assert len(result_empty_any.data["candidates"]) > 0
    assert len(result_empty_all.data["candidates"]) > 0
    assert len(result_empty_domain.data["candidates"]) > 0
```

```python
def test_search_total_matches_with_filters(kb_corpus):
    """Test that total_matches correctly accounts for filters."""
    tool = SemanticSearchTool(kb_corpus)
    # Filter by price should reduce total_matches
    result_all = tool(query="keyboard")
    result_filtered = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"price_max": 20})
    assert result_all.ok and result_filtered.ok
    assert result_filtered.data["total_matches"] <= result_all.data["total_matches"]
```

```python
def test_search_rejects_non_dict_filters(kb_corpus):
    """Test that non-dictionary filters are rejected."""
    tool = SemanticSearchTool(kb_corpus)
    result_list = tool(query="keyboard", filters=["domain"])
    result_str = tool(query="keyboard", filters="domain")
    result_int = tool(query="keyboard", filters=123)
    assert not result_list.ok
    assert "dictionary" in result_list.error
    assert not result_str.ok
    assert "dictionary" in result_str.error
    assert not result_int.ok
    assert "dictionary" in result_int.error
```

```python
def test_search_validates_filter_types(kb_corpus):
    """Test that filter values are validated for correct types."""
    tool = SemanticSearchTool(kb_corpus)
    # Invalid price filter
    result_bad_price = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"price_min": "invalid"})
    assert not result_bad_price.ok
    assert "price_min" in result_bad_price.error
    # Invalid rating_number filter
    result_bad_rating = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"min_rating_number": "abc"})
```

```python
def test_search_applied_filters_are_normalized(kb_corpus):
    """Test that applied_filters returns normalized filters, not raw input."""
    tool = SemanticSearchTool(kb_corpus)
    result = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"price_min": "50", "price_max": "100"})
    assert result.ok
    # Normalized filters should have numeric types, not strings
    applied = result.data["applied_filters"]
    assert isinstance(applied.get("price_min"), (int, float))
    assert isinstance(applied.get("price_max"), (int, float))
    assert applied["price_min"] == 50.0
    assert applied["price_max"] == 100.0
```

```python
    # Query for "keyboard" with categories_any filter

    assert "Electronics::k2" not in ids  # Wireless Gaming Mouse (Mice category)
    assert "Video_Games::g1" not in ids  # Keyboard Typing Adventure Game (Games category)
```

```python
def test_composite_index_exists_after_corpus_init():
    """Test that the composite index idx_cat_item_cat is created during corpus initialization."""
    meta = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "parent_asin": ["i1"],
            "domain": ["Electronics"],
            "title": ["Test Item"],
            "price": [10.0],
            "average_rating": [4.0],
            "rating_number": [100],
            "store": ["TestStore"],
            "main_category": ["TestCat"],
            "categories": [["Cat1", "Cat2"]],
        }
    )
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)
    # Check that the composite index exists
    indexes = corpus._conn.execute(
        "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='index' AND name='idx_cat_item_cat'"
    ).fetchall()
    assert len(indexes) == 1, "Composite index idx_cat_item_cat should exist after corpus initialization"
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/tools/retrieval/test_semantic_search_regressions.py::test_search_fts_with_categories_any_correctness_large_corpus`  (0.0147s)

_Correctness test: FTS + categories_any on a larger synthetic corpus._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_search_fts_with_categories_any_correctness_large_corpus():
    """Correctness test: FTS + categories_any on a larger synthetic corpus."""
    # Create a corpus with many items in a large category
    items = []
    for i in range(100):
        items.append({
            "parent_asin": f"item_{i}",
            "domain": "Electronics",
            "title": f"Test Item {i} keyboard",
            "price": 10.0 + i,
            "average_rating": 4.0,
            "rating_number": 100,
            "store": "TestStore",
            "main_category": "Keyboards",
            "categories": ["Keyboards"] if i < 90 else ["Other"],  # 90 items in Keyboards, 10 in Other
        })
    meta = pd.DataFrame(items)
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)
    tool = SemanticSearchTool(corpus)

    # Query with FTS + categories_any targeting the large category
    result = tool(query="keyboard", filters={"categories_any": ["Keyboards"]}, limit=50)
    assert result.ok
    # Should return results from the large category
    assert len(result.data["candidates"]) > 0
    # All returned items should be in Keyboards category
    for candidate in result.data["candidates"]:
        assert candidate["main_category"] == "Keyboards"
    # Total matches should reflect the filter
    assert result.data["total_matches"] >= 90  # At least the 90 items in Keyboards
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/tools/retrieval/test_semantic_search_regressions.py::test_categories_any_uses_composite_index`  (0.0096s)

_Regression test: FTS + categories_any must use idx_cat_item_cat index._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_categories_any_uses_composite_index():
    """Regression test: FTS + categories_any must use idx_cat_item_cat index.

    This test checks the query plan to ensure the composite index is used,
    which prevents timeouts on large corpora. Without the composite index,
    SQLite uses idx_cat_cat and repeatedly scans category entries for each FTS match.
    """
    meta = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "parent_asin": ["k1", "k2", "k3"],
            "domain": ["Electronics", "Electronics", "Electronics"],
            "title": ["Keyboard A", "Keyboard B", "Mouse"],
            "price": [10.0, 20.0, 15.0],
            "average_rating": [4.0, 4.5, 4.2],
            "rating_number": [100, 200, 150],
            "store": ["StoreA", "StoreB", "StoreC"],
            "main_category": ["Keyboards", "Keyboards", "Mice"],
            "categories": [["Keyboards"], ["Keyboards"], ["Mice"]],
        }
    )
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    # Run EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN on the same SQL pattern that SemanticSearchTool uses
    match = '"keyboard"'
    where = """EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM item_categories c WHERE c.item_id = items.item_id
AND c.category IN (?))"""
    sql = (
        f"SELECT items.item_id FROM items_fts "
        f"JOIN items ON items.rowid = items_fts.rowid "
        f"WHERE items_fts MATCH ? AND {where}"
    )
    plan = corpus._conn.execute(f"EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN {sql}", [match, "Keyboards"]).fetchall()

    # Check that the plan uses the composite index idx_cat_item_cat
    plan_str = " ".join(str(row) for row in plan)
    assert "idx_cat_item_cat" in plan_str, (
        f"Query plan should use composite index idx_cat_item_cat, but got:\n{plan}"
    )
    # Also verify it's a covering index (more efficient)
    assert "COVERING INDEX" in plan_str or "USING COVERING INDEX" in plan_str, (
        f"Query plan should use covering index for efficiency, but got:\n{plan}"
    )
```

```python
def test_item_cf_ranks_by_hand_computed_similarity_with_index_tie_break(cf_tool):
    # cos(A1,.): B2 and T_A1 tie at 4/sqrt(20)=0.894 (lower index first), E5 3/sqrt(15)=0.775, C3 2/sqrt(10)=0.632, D4 1/sqrt(5)=0.447
    result = cf_tool(items=[G_A1])
    assert [c["item_id"] for c in result.data["candidates"]] == [G_B2, T_A1, T_E5, G_C3, G_D4]
    assert [round(c["similarity"], 3) for c in result.data["candidates"]] == [0.894, 0.894, 0.775, 0.632, 0.447]
    assert [c["domain"] for c in result.data["candidates"]] == ["Games", "Toys", "Toys", "Games", "Games"]
    assert G_A1 not in [c["item_id"] for c in result.data["candidates"]]  # seed excluded by default
```

```python
def test_reco_ranks_unseen_candidates_by_hand_computed_scores(reco_tool):
    # u2 saw {A1,B2,T_A1,T_E5}; unseen candidates C3 and D4 get sum of similarities to the history:
    # C3: .632+.707+.707+.816 = 2.867 (hand: 2/sqrt10 + 2/sqrt8 + 2/sqrt8 + 2/sqrt6)  D4: .447+.5+.5+.577
    result = reco_tool(user_id="u2", candidates=[G_B2, T_A1, T_E5, G_C3, G_D4])
    d = result.data
    assert [r["item_id"] for r in d["ranked"]] == [G_C3, G_D4]
    c3 = 2 / 10 ** 0.5 + 2 / 8 ** 0.5 + 2 / 8 ** 0.5 + 2 / 6 ** 0.5
    d4 = 1 / 5 ** 0.5 + 1 / 4 ** 0.5 + 1 / 4 ** 0.5 + 1 / 3 ** 0.5
    assert d["ranked"][0]["score"] == pytest.approx(c3, abs=1e-4) and d["ranked"][1]["score"] == pytest.approx(d4, abs=1e-4)
    assert d["excluded_seen"] == [G_B2, T_A1, T_E5] and d["cold_start"] is False and d["has_personal_signal"] is True
    assert [r["domain"] for r in d["ranked"]] == ["Games", "Games"]
```

```python
    result = reco_tool(user_id="stranger", candidates=[G_D4, G_C3, T_E5, G_A1])
    assert [r["item_id"] for r in result.data["ranked"]] == [G_A1, T_E5, G_C3, G_D4]  # popularity 5,3,2,1
    assert result.data["cold_start"] is True and result.data["has_personal_signal"] is False
```

```python
def test_reco_ties_keep_candidate_input_order(world):
    # B2 and T_A1 have equal popularity (4): the order of the input decides, deterministically
    tool = RecoModelTool(BaselineScorer(PopularityRecommender().fit(world.interactions)), world.user2id, world.item2id)
    one = [r["item_id"] for r in tool(user_id="stranger", candidates=[G_B2, T_A1]).data["ranked"]]
    two = [r["item_id"] for r in tool(user_id="stranger", candidates=[T_A1, G_B2]).data["ranked"]]
```

```python
def test_item_cf_sums_similarity_over_multiple_seeds(corpus, cf_setup):
    interactions, _, item2id = cf_setup
    tool = ItemCFTool(ItemKNNRecommender(5).fit(interactions), item2id, corpus=corpus)
    single = {c["item_id"]: c["similarity"] for c in tool(items=[VG_A]).data["candidates"]}
    multi = {c["item_id"]: c["similarity"] for c in tool(items=[VG_A, EL_D]).data["candidates"]}
    assert multi[EL_A] > single[EL_A]  # both seeds contribute to EL_A
```

```python
def test_item_cf_reports_unknown_seeds(corpus, cf_setup):
    interactions, _, item2id = cf_setup
    tool = ItemCFTool(ItemKNNRecommender(5).fit(interactions), item2id, corpus=corpus)

    assert only_unknown.ok and only_unknown.data["candidates"] == []
    assert only_unknown.data["unknown_items"] == ["Video_Games::zzz"]
```

```python
def test_item_cf_input_validation(corpus, cf_setup):
    interactions, _, item2id = cf_setup
    knn = ItemKNNRecommender(5).fit(interactions)
    assert not ItemCFTool(knn, item2id, corpus=corpus)(items=[]).ok
    assert not ItemCFTool(knn, item2id, corpus=corpus)(items=[VG_A], top_k=0).ok
```

```python
def test_item_cf_negative_index_in_mapping_is_an_unknown_seed(knn, cf_setup, corpus):
    """-1 would silently select the LAST row of the similarity matrix (numpy negative indexing)."""
    _, _, item2id = cf_setup
    tool = ItemCFTool(knn, {**item2id, "Fake::neg": -1}, corpus=corpus)
    result = tool(items=["Fake::neg"])
    assert result.ok
    assert result.data["candidates"] == []
    assert result.data["unknown_items"] == ["Fake::neg"]
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/tools/ranking/test_ranking_hardening.py::test_item_cf_negative_seed_does_not_pollute_valid_seeds`  (0.0111s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_item_cf_negative_seed_does_not_pollute_valid_seeds(knn, cf_setup, corpus):
    _, _, item2id = cf_setup
    tool = ItemCFTool(knn, {**item2id, "Fake::neg": -1}, corpus=corpus)
    mixed = tool(items=[VG_A, "Fake::neg"])
    clean = ItemCFTool(knn, item2id, corpus=corpus)(items=[VG_A])
    assert _ids(mixed) == _ids(clean)
    assert [c["similarity"] for c in mixed.data["candidates"]] == [c["similarity"] for c in clean.data["candidates"]]
    assert mixed.data["unknown_items"] == ["Fake::neg"]
```

```python
def test_item_cf_seed_index_beyond_similarity_matrix_is_unknown(knn, cf_setup, corpus):

    n = knn.similarity.shape[0]
    tool = ItemCFTool(knn, {**item2id, "Fake::big": n + 5}, corpus=corpus)

    assert result.ok and result.data["candidates"] == [] and result.data["unknown_items"] == ["Fake::big"]
```

```python
def test_item_cf_rejects_mapping_where_two_items_share_an_index(knn, cf_setup):
```

```python
def test_reco_negative_index_in_mapping_goes_to_unscored(cf_setup, knn):
    """idx -1 passes `idx >= num_items` and would be 'scored' (as 0.0) and ranked."""
    _, user2id, item2id = cf_setup
    tool = RecoModelTool(BaselineScorer(knn), user2id, {**item2id, "Fake::neg": -1})
    result = tool(user_id="u3", candidates=[VG_B, "Fake::neg"])
    assert result.ok
    assert "Fake::neg" in result.data["unscored_item_ids"]
    assert "Fake::neg" not in _ranked_ids(result)
    assert VG_B in _ranked_ids(result)
```

```python
def test_item_cf_exclude_input_applies_to_every_seed(cf_tool):
    """EL_A is a neighbour of VG_A, but it is also a seed -> must be excluded; with the flag off it comes back."""
    excluded = cf_tool(items=[VG_A, EL_A], exclude_input=True)
    assert set(_ids(excluded)) == {VG_B, EL_D}
    kept = cf_tool(items=[VG_A, EL_A], exclude_input=False)
    assert {VG_A, EL_A} <= set(_ids(kept))
```

```python
def test_item_cf_empty_domain_note_does_not_claim_filtering_after_top_k(cf_tool):
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/tools/ranking/test_ranking_hardening.py::test_reco_never_returns_a_non_finite_score[nan]`  (0.001s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", [np.nan, np.inf, -np.inf], ids=["nan", "inf", "-inf"])
def test_reco_never_returns_a_non_finite_score(bad):
    tool = _fake_tool(lambda idx: np.where(idx == 1, bad, 0.5))
    result = tool(user_id="u", candidates=["i0", "i1", "i2"])
    if result.ok:  # either the item is dropped / reported, or the call fails cleanly -- never a NaN in `ranked`
        assert all(math.isfinite(r["score"]) for r in result.data["ranked"])
        assert "i1" not in _ranked_ids(result)
    else:
```

_a only touched X, and nobody else touched X -> the model knows a, but X has no similar items._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_known_user_without_neighbours_is_not_cold_start_but_has_no_personal_signal():
    """a only touched X, and nobody else touched X -> the model knows a, but X has no similar items."""
    inter = pd.DataFrame({"user_id": ["a", "b", "b", "c", "c"], "item_id": ["X", "Y", "Z", "Y", "Z"]})
    user2id = {u: i for i, u in enumerate(inter["user_id"].unique())}
    item2id = {it: i for i, it in enumerate(inter["item_id"].unique())}
    inter["user_idx"] = inter["user_id"].map(user2id)
    inter["item_idx"] = inter["item_id"].map(item2id)
    tool = RecoModelTool(BaselineScorer(ItemKNNRecommender(5).fit(inter)), user2id, item2id)

    known_isolated = tool(user_id="a", candidates=["Y", "Z"])
    assert known_isolated.ok
    assert known_isolated.data["cold_start"] is False
    assert known_isolated.data["has_personal_signal"] is False

    with_signal = tool(user_id="b", candidates=["X", "Y"], exclude_seen=False)
    assert with_signal.data["cold_start"] is False and with_signal.data["has_personal_signal"] is True

    stranger = tool(user_id="nobody", candidates=["X", "Y"])
    assert stranger.data["cold_start"] is True and stranger.data["has_personal_signal"] is False
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/tools/ranking/test_ranking_hardening.py::test_reco_partitions_candidates_into_ranked_seen_and_unscored[True-u1]`  (0.0098s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("user", ["u1", "u2", "u3", "u4", "u5", "u6", "u7", "u8", "u9", "u10", "u13", "brand_new_user"])
@pytest.mark.parametrize("exclude_seen", [True, False])
def test_reco_partitions_candidates_into_ranked_seen_and_unscored(reco_tool, user, exclude_seen):
    result = reco_tool(user_id=user, candidates=ALL_CANDIDATES, exclude_seen=exclude_seen)
    assert result.ok
    ranked, seen, unscored = _ranked_ids(result), result.data["excluded_seen"], result.data["unscored_item_ids"]
    buckets = ranked + seen + unscored
    assert sorted(buckets) == sorted(ALL_CANDIDATES), "a candidate was lost or duplicated"
    scores = [r["score"] for r in result.data["ranked"]]
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/tools/ranking/test_reco_model_tool.py::test_reco_accepts_other_tools_output_directly`  (0.014s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_reco_accepts_other_tools_output_directly(corpus, cf_setup):
    interactions, user2id, item2id = cf_setup
    knn = ItemKNNRecommender(5).fit(interactions)
    cf = ItemCFTool(knn, item2id, corpus=corpus)
    reco = RecoModelTool(BaselineScorer(knn), user2id, item2id, corpus=corpus)
    candidates = cf(items=[VG_A]).data["candidates"]
    assert reco(user_id="u3", candidates=candidates).ok
    sql_candidates = SQLTool(corpus)().data["candidates"]
    assert reco(user_id="u3", candidates=sql_candidates).ok
```

```text

    0      u1  Video_Games::vg_a         0         0
    1      u1  Electronics::el_a         0         1
    2      u2  Video_Games::vg_a         1         0
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/tools/ranking/test_reco_model_tool.py::test_reco_items_outside_model_matrix_go_to_unscored`  (0.0061s)

_Items in item2id with idx >= num_items (outside model matrix) must be unscored, not scored as 0.0._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_reco_items_outside_model_matrix_go_to_unscored(cf_setup):
    """Items in item2id with idx >= num_items (outside model matrix) must be unscored, not scored as 0.0."""
    interactions, user2id, item2id = cf_setup
    # Create a tool with a popularity model
    model = PopularityRecommender().fit(interactions)
    # Add a fake item to item2id with idx >= num_items (simulating item added after model training)
    fake_item = "Fake::item_outside_matrix"
    fake_idx = model.num_items + 10  # Definitely outside the model matrix
    item2id_extended = {**item2id, fake_item: fake_idx}
    tool = RecoModelTool(BaselineScorer(model), user2id, item2id_extended)
    result = tool(user_id="u1", candidates=[VG_B, fake_item])
    assert result.ok
    # The fake item should be in unscored_item_ids, not in ranked with score 0.0
    assert fake_item in result.data["unscored_item_ids"]
    assert VG_B in [r["item_id"] for r in result.data["ranked"]]
    assert fake_item not in [r["item_id"] for r in result.data["ranked"]]
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/tools/ranking/test_reco_model_tool.py::test_reco_has_personal_signal_flag`  (0.0088s)

_has_personal_signal distinguishes users with CF signal from those with only popularity._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_reco_has_personal_signal_flag(cf_setup):
    """has_personal_signal distinguishes users with CF signal from those with only popularity."""
    interactions, user2id, item2id = cf_setup
    # u2 has history and CF neighbors, so should have personal signal
    knn = ItemKNNRecommender(5).fit(interactions)
    tool_knn = RecoModelTool(BaselineScorer(knn), user2id, item2id)
    result = tool_knn(user_id="u2", candidates=[VG_A, EL_A])
    assert result.ok
    assert result.data["has_personal_signal"] is True

    # Cold start user should not have personal signal
    result_cold = tool_knn(user_id="brand_new_user", candidates=[VG_A, EL_A])
    assert result_cold.ok
    assert result_cold.data["has_personal_signal"] is False

    # Popularity model never has personal signal
    model = PopularityRecommender().fit(interactions)
    tool_pop = RecoModelTool(BaselineScorer(model), user2id, item2id)
    result_pop = tool_pop(user_id="u2", candidates=[VG_A, EL_A])
    assert result_pop.ok
    assert result_pop.data["has_personal_signal"] is False
```

```python
def test_reco_note_when_all_candidates_seen(corpus, cf_setup):
    """When all candidates have been seen, ranked=[] and note explains why."""
    tool = _reco(corpus, cf_setup)
    # u2 has seen VG_A and VG_B; pass only these with exclude_seen=True
    result = tool(user_id="u2", candidates=[VG_A, VG_B], exclude_seen=True)
```
